\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{1},p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2},s_{1}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\Big].

(62)

The same-sign integrals, however, are a bit more complicated owing to the Heaviside
\theta
functions for the (anti-)time-orderings. There are multiple ways to deal with this time ordering. It turns that different treatments will yield results with different convergent speeds, and the convergence speed also depends on whether
k_{12}>k_{34}
or
k_{12}<k_{34}
.

Assuming
k_{12}>k_{34}
(and thus
r_{1}<r_{2}
) for the moment. It turns out useful to adopt the following rearrangement of the bulk propagator
D_{\pm\pm}
:

\displaystyle D_{\pm\pm}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~D_{\gtrless}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})+\Big[D_{\lessgtr}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})-D_{\gtrless}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big]\theta(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}).

(63)

Correspondingly, we rewrite the seed integral
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
as:

\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).~~~~(r_{1}<r_{2})

(64)

Here the factorized (F) integral
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
and the time-ordered (TO) integral
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
are respectively defined as: (The subscript
>
associated with these integrals serves as a reminder that we are focusing on the region
k_{12}>k_{34}
.)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle-k_{s}^{5+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}(k_{12}\tau_{1}+k_{34}\tau_{2})}D_{\gtrless}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(65)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle-k_{s}^{5+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\tau_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}(k_{12}\tau_{1}+k_{34}\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle~\times\Big[D_{\lessgtr}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})-D_{\gtrless}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big].

(66)

Then, the factorized time integral, very similar to the opposite-sign integrals (
62
), can be carried out directly:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4\pi}e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}r_{1}^{5/2+p_{1}}r_{2}^{5/2+p_{2}}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,(\pm\mathrm{i}e^{\pm 2\mathrm{i}\pi s_{1}})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{1}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{1},p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2},s_{1}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\Big],

(67)

The time-ordered integral can also be carried out with the aid of (
A
):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4\pi}e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}r_{1}^{5+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,(\mp\mathrm{i}e^{\pm 2\mathrm{i}\pi s_{1}}\pm\mathrm{i}e^{\pm 2\mathrm{i}\pi s_{2}})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{12}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2},p_{1}+p_{2}+5-2s_{12},s_{1}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\Big]

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2},p_{1}+p_{2}+5-2s_{12}\\
p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{7}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2}\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\right].

(68)

Here
{}_{2}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}
is the regularized hypergeometric function. See (
276
) for the definition.

It turns out that the above results obtained with the assumption of
k_{12}>k_{34}
are also valid when
k_{12}<k_{34}
. We suspect the reason is that our result for the background has resummed all powers of
k_{34}/k_{12}
into a well behaved function which is analytic at
k_{12}=k_{34}
, so that the series of
r_{1}/r_{2}
originally defined in the range of
k_{12}>k_{34}
is analytically continued to the opposite side
k_{12}<k_{34}
. However, we also observe that the convergence of the
r_{1}
series in (
68
) becomes slow as
r_{1}\rightarrow 1
.

Alternatively, we can also perform the calculation again with the assumption
k_{12}<k_{34}
from the very beginning. For instance, we can also define the factorized integral
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},<}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
and the time-ordered integral
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},<}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
. The results can be simply obtained by switching the variables
(r_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(r_{2},p_{2})
. In practice, it turns out that the integrals expressed in the following way would have fast convergence speed for all physical range of parameters:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(69)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\theta(r_{2}-r_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{{p_{2}p_{1}}}(r_{2},r_{1})\theta(r_{1}-r_{2}),

(70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\theta(r_{2}-r_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{{p_{2}p_{1}}}(r_{2},r_{1})\theta(r_{1}-r_{2}).

(71)

Signal and background.

We are now ready to carry out the MB integrals in (
62
), (
67
) and (
68
), by closing the contours and picking up the enclosed poles. Again let us focus on the case of
k_{12}>k_{34}
. In this region we have
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<1
, which shows that we should close the contour from left for each of the integrals. Then, all the enclosed poles are from the
\Gamma
functions of the form
\Gamma(s_{1,2}\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu/2)
, which are called “left poles.” These poles can be divided into two sets:

\displaystyle s_{1}=-n_{1}\mp\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},\quad s_{2}=-n_{2}\pm\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},

(72)

\displaystyle s_{1}=-n_{1}\mp\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},\quad s_{2}=-n_{2}\mp\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}.

(73)

Then using the residue theorem, we can complete the integrals.
The integrals (
62
) and (
67
) can be combined together:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{S},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{+-}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{++,\text{F},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\text{c.c.}.

(74)

More explicitly,

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{S},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4\pi}r_{1}^{5/2+p_{1}}r_{2}^{5/2+p_{2}}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,\Big[e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}-p_{2})/2}+\mathrm{i}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}e^{2\mathrm{i}\pi s_{1}}\Big]\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{1}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{1},p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-2s_{2},s_{1}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\Big]+\text{c.c.}.

(75)

The result of this integral is obtained by summing over the residues of all the poles listed in (
72
) and (
73
). It is clear that the first set of poles (
72
) contribute to a result proportional to
(r_{1}/r_{2})^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
, which is the local (L) signal, while the second set (
73
) leads to the nonlocal (NL) signal of the form
(r_{1}r_{2})^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
. For positive real
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
, it is clear that the local and nonlocal signals exhibit oscillatory behavior in
\log(r_{1}/r_{2})
and
\log(r_{1}r_{2})
, respectively, and this is why they are called “signals.” For phenomenological applications, it would be useful to spell out these signals more clearly. Therefore, we make the following rearrangement:

{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{S},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})={\mathcal{I}}_{\text{L},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{NL},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(76)

in which the local-signal integral is:

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{L},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4\pi}r_{1}^{5/2+p_{1}}r_{2}^{5/2+p_{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}\Big[e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}-p_{2})/2}+\mathrm{i}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big]\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[2n_{1}+p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-n_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigg\}+\text{c.c.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.}.

(77)

Here we have defined

\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p}(r)\equiv r^{5/2+p}\Gamma\Big[\displaystyle\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}+p+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{7}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,r^{2}\right],

(78)

and

\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\equiv\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2\pi}\Big[\cos\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}-p_{2})\big)+\sin\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}+p_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big)\Big].

(79)

Similarly, the nonlocal signal can be worked out as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{NL},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{4}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.}.

(80)

It then remains to compute the time-ordered integral (
68
). It can be seen that the second set of poles (
73
) do not contribute to the result, so that we can consider the first set of poles (
72
) only. (The regularized hypergeometric function is regular with respect to all the three orders and thus contributes no poles.) The result is thus analytic in
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
at
r_{1}=r_{2}=0
, and corresponds to the “EFT” part, or the “background” (BG) part, of the correlator. (Again, see
[
35
]
for explanations of the background part.) Therefore, we can write:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{BG},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv{\mathcal{I}}_{++,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}_{--,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(81)

and more explicitly,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{BG},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cos\big[\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}+p_{2})\big]r_{1}^{5+p_{1}+p_{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{12}}(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{1}}(\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{F}}\left[\begin{matrix}2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,2n_{12}+p_{1}+p_{2}+5\\
2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{7}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\right]+\text{c.c.},

(82)

where we have used the Pochhammer symbol
(z)_{n}\equiv\Gamma(z+n)/\Gamma(z)
and the “dressed” hypergeometric function
\mathcal{F}
. See (
277
) for the definition.
Thus we have finished the computation of the scalar seed integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
for
r_{1}<r_{2}
. The result for
r_{1}>r_{2}
is obtained by switching
(r_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(r_{2},p_{2})
in all the results above. We note that the nonlocal signal (
80
) is symmetric with respect to
(r_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(r_{2},p_{2})
, so that we can simply drop the subscript “
>
”:

{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{NL}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})={\mathcal{I}}_{\text{NL},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(83)

On the other hand, the local signal (
77
) and the background (
82
) are asymmetric.

3.3
Summary

Now we summarize the result for the scalar seed integral obtained above. As we have seen, the scalar seed integral can be expressed as a function of two independent momentum ratios
r_{1}=k_{s}/k_{12}
and
r_{2}=k_{s}/k_{34}
. The following expressions apply to the case of
r_{1}<r_{2}
. The results for
r_{1}>r_{2}
can be obtained from the following expressions simply by switching the variables
(r_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(r_{2},p_{2})
.

For convenience, we only present the scalar seed integral with all SK indices summed. According to the analytic properties at
r_{1}=0
and
r_{2}=0
, the summed integral can be put into the following three terms, respectively called the background (BG), the local signal (L), and the nonlocal signal (NL):

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}=\pm}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})

(84)

The background piece
\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
is analytic in both
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
as
r_{1},r_{2}\rightarrow 0
, and therefore possesses a power-series representation. The local signal
\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
is nonanalytic in
r_{1}/r_{2}
as the ratio reaches 0, but is analytic in
k_{s}
as
k_{s}\rightarrow 0
. Finally, the nonlocal signal
\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
is nonanalytic in
r_{1}r_{2}
as
r_{1}r_{2}\rightarrow 0
, and thus is also nonanalytic in
k_{s}
as
k_{s}\rightarrow 0
. The explicit expressions for these integrals are summarized as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cos\big[\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}+p_{2})\big]r_{1}^{5+p_{1}+p_{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{12}}(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{1}}(\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{F}}\left[\begin{matrix}2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,2n_{12}+p_{1}+p_{2}+5\\
2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{7}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\right]+\text{c.c.}

(85)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.},

(86)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{4}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.}.

(87)

Here
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu=\sqrt{m^{2}/H^{2}-9/4}
is the mass parameter of the intermediate massive scalar, which we always assume real.
(z)_{n}
is the Pochhammer symbol, defined in (
274
),
\mathcal{F}
is the dressed hypergeometric function, defined in (
277
). The function
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p}
is defined in (
78
) and the coefficient
\mathcal{C}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
is defined in (
79
).

4  Helical Spin-1 Exchange

In this section we compute the vector seed integral (
43
) using the partial MB representation, which leads to the main result of this paper, namely an exact and analytical expression for the helical 4-point correlators. This section will be rather technical. Readers uninterested in the computation details are invited to go directly to Sec.
4.3
for a summary of the result.

The vector seed integral (
43
) is defined for each intermediate polarization, labeled by
\lambda=\pm,L
. As one can see from (
22
) and (
24
), the two transverse polarizations with
\lambda=\pm
are quite different from the longitudinal polarization with
\lambda=L
. Therefore we shall treat these two cases separately, with the transverse integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(\pm)p_{1}p_{2}}
computed in Sec.
4.1
and the longitudinal integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}
in Sec.
4.2
.

4.1
Transverse integral

We now use the partial Mellin transformation to carry out the transverse integral
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{({\pm})p_{1}p_{2}}
. As one can see from (
10
), for the two transverse components, the helicity basis is in one-to-one relationship with the polarization basis, and therefore we are free to use the helicity label
(h)
to replace the polarization label
(\lambda)
in various expressions.

To perform the partial Mellin transformation on the transverse mode function (
22
), we could use the following Mellin-Barnes representation for the Whittaker function, which we call the completely resolved representation
[
110
]
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{\kappa,\nu}(az)=\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{+\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}s-\nu,s+\nu\\
s-\kappa+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\end{matrix}\middle|\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\right](az)^{-s+1/2},

(88)

where
\mathcal{F}
is the dressed hypergeometric function defined in (
277
). The advantage of this representation is that all
z
-dependences in the Whittaker function have been completely resolved into power functions, which trivializes the time integral. The drawback, however, is that the integrand involves a hypergeometric function. Although this posts no essential difficulty for us to perform the contour integral, the resulting expression has a somewhat more complicated form. We will present the result derived from this completely resolved representation in App.
C
. Here, instead, we make use of the fact that the Whittaker function, when multiplied by an appropriate exponential factor, has a far simpler Mellin-Barnes representation. There are two ways to add this exponential factor, which lead to the following two alternative results, which we call partially resolved representations
[
110
]
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{\kappa,\nu}(az)=

\displaystyle~e^{az/2}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{+\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,\Gamma\left[\begin{matrix}s-\nu,s+\nu\\
s-\kappa+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\end{matrix}\right](az)^{-s+1/2}.

(89)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{\kappa,\nu}(az)=

\displaystyle~e^{-az/2}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{+\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,\Gamma\left[\begin{matrix}s-\nu,s+\nu,-s-\kappa+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\kappa-\nu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\kappa+\nu\end{matrix}\right](az)^{-s+1/2}.

(90)

We have the freedom to use either of them when computing the seed integral so long as all parameters stay in the validity ranges of the above two representations. (See
[
110
]
for detailed specification of validity ranges of all MB representations quoted here.)

Opposite-sign integrals.

First consider the opposite-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\mp}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}
:

\mathcal{I}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\pm\mp}(r_{1},r_{2})=k_{s}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}k_{12}\tau_{1}\mp\mathrm{i}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D_{\pm\mp}^{(h)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(91)

The two time integrals in these expressions are already factorized and so the integration is straightforward. For a reason that will be clear soon, we choose (
90
) for the both Whittaker functions in
D_{\pm\mp}^{(h)}
:

\displaystyle D_{\pm\mp}^{(h)}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)e^{\pm\mathrm{i}k(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}-s_{2})/2}(2k_{s})^{-s_{12}}(-\tau_{1})^{-s_{1}+1/2}(-\tau_{2})^{-s_{2}+1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[-s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\mp\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big].

(92)

Inserting the MB representation into (
91
) and completing the time integrals, we obtain:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{1}}\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{2}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{2}}e^{\mp\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,u_{1}^{-s_{1}}u_{2}^{-s_{2}}\Gamma\big[p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s_{1},p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\big[-s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\mp\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big],

(93)

where we have used the previously defined momentum ratios
u_{1}=2r_{1}/(1+r_{1})
and
u_{2}=2r_{2}/(1+r_{2})
.

At this point we can explain why we choose (
90
) to represent the propagator: Had we chosen the other representation (
89
), the integral (
4.1
), and also all the subsequent results, would be expressed in terms of
w_{1}=2r_{1}/(1-r_{1})
and
w_{2}=2r_{2}/(1-r_{2})
instead of
u_{1,2}
. As we shall see below, the result of the vector seed integral involves the hypergeometric function
{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}
of the momentum ratio. We know that
{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}(a,b;c;z)
for generic orders
(a,b,c)
is singular at
z=1
. Thus, had we used (
89
) which gives rise to results in terms of
w_{1,2}
, we would encounter spurious divergences at
w_{1,2}=1
, namely
r_{1,2}=1/3
, a configuration of no special physical significance. Of course this superficial divergence must get canceled in the final result, but its presence would make the final expression rather ugly, and also make the numerical implementation hard. Therefore, here and below, we will always make choices from (
89
) and (
90
) such that we always get
u_{1,2}
instead of
w_{1,2}
in the final expressions.
5
5

5

Of course, our choice to represent the result in terms of
u_{1,2}
does not remove the singularity of the hypergeometric function at
u_{1,2}=1
. However,
u_{1,2}=1
, namely
r_{1,2}=1
, correspond to the folded limit of the momentum configuration, which does have a physical significance. Requiring no divergences at the folded limit corresponds to choosing the Bunch-Davies vacuum as the initial states for all the quantum fields.

Given
0<u_{1,2}<1
, we should close the contours of MB integrals in (
4.1
) from left with a large semicircle. Applying the residue theorem, we pick up the following left poles of the integrand:

\displaystyle s_{1}=-n_{1}\mp\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\quad s_{2}=-n_{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,

(94)

\displaystyle s_{1}=-n_{1}\mp\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\quad s_{2}=-n_{2}\mp\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu.

(95)

The result for the MB integral is thus obtained by summing over the residues of all the above left poles:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{1}}\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{2}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{2}}e^{\mp\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{1}}}\,}{n_{1}!}u_{1}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n_{1}}\Gamma\big[n_{1}+p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,n_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-n_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{2}}}\,}{n_{2}!}u_{2}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n_{2}}\Gamma\big[n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,n_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\mp\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-n_{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigg\}.

(96)

The summation can be done in closed form, and the result can be expressed as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)e^{\mp\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}

\displaystyle\times\Big[\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{\pm h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})u_{1}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{\pm h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})u_{1}^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big]\Big[\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{\mp h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})u_{2}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{\mp h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})u_{2}^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big],

(97)

where we have defined

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{p}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u)\equiv\mathrm{i}\pi\text{csch}(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u}\,}{2}\Big)^{3/2+p}\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+p+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|u\right].

(98)

Same-sign integrals: factorized part.

We focus on the double-plus integral
\mathcal{I}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++}
, and still considering the case of
k_{12}>k_{34}
. The result for
k_{12}<k_{34}
can be obtained by switching the variables
(u_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(u_{2},p_{2})
as before, and the result for
\mathcal{I}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{--}
can be obtained by taking complex conjugation.

Similar to the case of the scalar integral discussed in the previous section, the same-sign integral can be separated into a factorized part and a time-ordered part:

\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).~~~~(r_{1}<r_{2})

(99)

The factorized integral is given by:

\mathcal{I}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{F},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=-k_{s}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{+\mathrm{i}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D_{>}^{(h)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(100)

The computation of this integral is much similar to the opposite-sign integral, except that we should now use a different MB representation for the Wightman function
D_{>}
, since the exponential factor in (
100
) is
e^{+\mathrm{i}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}k_{34}\tau_{2}}
:

\displaystyle D_{>}^{(h)}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{+\mathrm{i}k(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}-s_{2})/2}\cos\pi(s_{1}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)(2k_{s})^{-s_{12}}(-\tau_{1})^{-s_{1}+1/2}(-\tau_{2})^{-s_{2}+1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[-s_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big].

(101)

Substituting this representation into (
100
) and carrying out the time integral, we get:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{F},>}=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma\big[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big]}\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{1}}\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{2}}\,}{2}\big)^{3/2+p_{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{+\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}+1/2)}\cos\pi(s_{1}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}^{-s_{1}}u_{2}^{-s_{2}}\Gamma\big[p_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s_{1},p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\big[-s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big].

(102)

Then, following the same procedure as before, we close the contour from the left, pick up all enclosed (left) poles, namely (
94
) and (
95
), and sum over the residues at these poles, to get the final result:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{F},>}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma\big[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big]}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}

\displaystyle\times\Big[e^{+\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(\nu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})u_{1}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(-\nu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})u_{1}^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big]

\displaystyle\times\Big[\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})u_{2}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})u_{2}^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big],

(103)

where
\mathbf{G}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p}(u)
is the same function as is defined in (
98
).

Local and nonlocal signals.

It is not surprising that the factorized part of the same-sign integral has very similar structure as the opposite-sign integrals. In particular, both results are nonanalytic in
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
as
u_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
. Similar to the case of scalar integral, these two parts contribute to the local and nonlocal signals:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{++,\text{F},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{I}_{--,\text{F},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{I}_{+-}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{I}_{-+}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}=\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}},

(104)

with the local signal
\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}
and the nonlocal signal
\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}
given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{G}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{L}}(u_{1},u_{2})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\text{c.c.}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{G}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{NL}}(u_{1},u_{2})(u_{1}u_{2})^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\text{c.c.}

(105)

where we have defined:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\text{L}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-\pi h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\bigg\{\big(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)\Big[e^{-\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})

\displaystyle+e^{+\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle~+\displaystyle\frac{\,{-2\mathrm{i}\pi e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}\,}{\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{-\mathrm{i}(p_{1}+p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})

\displaystyle~+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2\mathrm{i}\pi e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}\,}{\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{+\mathrm{i}(p_{1}+p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})\bigg\},

(106)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\text{NL}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-\pi h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2\pi^{2}}\bigg\{\big(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)\Big[e^{-\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})

\displaystyle+e^{+\mathrm{i}(p_{1}-p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle~+\displaystyle\frac{\,{-2\mathrm{i}\pi e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}\,}{\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{-\mathrm{i}(p_{1}+p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})

\displaystyle~+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2\mathrm{i}\pi e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}\,}{\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{+\mathrm{i}(p_{1}+p_{2})\pi/2}\mathbf{G}^{p_{1}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\mathbf{G}^{p_{2}}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{2})\bigg\}.

(107)

Note that the nonlocal signal is symmetric with respect to
(u_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(u_{2},p_{2})
, and thus the result applies well to the other side
k_{12}<k_{34}
. So, we do not put a subscript
>
for the nonlocal signal.

Same-sign integrals: time-ordered part.

Now we come to the time-ordered part:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{++,\text{TO},>}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv

\displaystyle-k_{s}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\tau_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}(k_{12}\tau_{1}+k_{34}\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle~\times\Big[D_{<}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})-D_{>}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big].

(108)

We use the following MB representations for the Wightman functions
D_{>}
and
D_{<}
to ensure that all results are expressed in terms of
u_{1,2}
:

\displaystyle D_{>}^{(h)}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{+\mathrm{i}k(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}-s_{2})/2}\cos\pi(s_{1}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)(2k)^{-s_{12}}(-\tau_{1})^{-s_{1}+1/2}(-\tau_{2})^{-s_{2}+1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[-s_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big],

(109)

\displaystyle D_{<}^{(h)}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{+\mathrm{i}k(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}-s_{2})/2}\cos\pi(s_{2}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)(2k)^{-s_{12}}(-\tau_{1})^{-s_{1}+1/2}(-\tau_{2})^{-s_{2}+1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[-s_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big].

(110)

Substituting these MB representations into the time-ordered integral (
4.1
), we can finish the time integral to get the following result:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{TO},>}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{\pi\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}}\,}{2^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}

\displaystyle\times\Big[e^{\mathrm{i}\pi s_{2}}\cos\pi(s_{2}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)-e^{\mathrm{i}\pi s_{1}}\cos\pi(s_{1}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big]u_{1}^{-s_{12}+3+p_{1}+p_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Big[-s_{1}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-s_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,s_{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-s_{2},p_{1}+p_{2}+3-s_{12}\\
p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-s_{2}\end{matrix}\middle|-\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{u_{2}}\right],

(111)

where
\mathcal{F}
is the dressed hypergeometric function defined in (
277
). The function
\mathcal{F}
does possess poles in
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
but they are right poles, and thus do not contribute to the result, since we should close the contour from the left. (Notice that the contribution from the poles in (
95
) vanishes.) The result of the time-ordered integral is fully analytic in
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
at
u_{1,2}=0
(when
p_{1}+p_{2}
are integers no smaller than
-3
), as opposed to the opposite-sign integral and the factorized same-sign integral. Thus the time-ordered integral exactly gives rise to the background piece of correlator. Let us write

{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{BG},>}={\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{TO},>}+{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{--,\text{TO},>}={\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{++,\text{TO},>}+\text{c.c.}.

(112)

Then the result for the background integral can be directly obtained as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{BG},>}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}}\,}{2^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}u_{1}^{n_{12}+3+p_{1}+p_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,}{2\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)_{n_{1}}\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)_{n_{2}}\big(-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)_{-n_{1}}\big(+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)_{-n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{F}}\left[\begin{matrix}n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,n_{12}+p_{1}+p_{2}+3\\
n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{u_{2}}\right]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\bigg\}+\text{c.c.}.

(113)

4.2
Longitudinal integral

Next we consider the longitudinal component (
\lambda=L
) of the vector seed integral (
43
). The longitudinal polarization is independent of the chemical potential, and the mode function is quite similar to that of a massive scalar field. The computation thus follows exactly the same procedure as in Sec.
3
, and is also relatively easier than the transverse integral. Therefore we will not spell out all the details, but only present main steps and the final results.

The longitudinal vector seed integral reads:

{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1},r_{2})=-\mathsf{ab}k_{s}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}\,(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D_{\mathsf{ab}}^{(L)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(114)

Comparing (
35
), (
36
), and (
50
), we see that the longitudinal propagator
D^{(L)}_{>}
is related to the massive scalar propagator
D_{>}
of the same mass parameter
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
(but not the same mass) via:

\displaystyle D_{>}^{(L)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}}\Big(\partial_{\tau_{1}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{2}\,}{\tau_{1}}\Big)\Big(\partial_{\tau_{2}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{2}\,}{\tau_{2}}\Big)D_{>}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(115)

where we have explicitly expressed the mass as
m^{2}=\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4
. Therefore we can recycle the MB representation for the scalar propagator, which leads us to the following expression:

\displaystyle D_{\lessgtr}^{(L)}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$})}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}}\,}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(s_{1}-s_{2})}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{k}\,}{2}\Big)^{-2s_{12}}(-\tau_{1})^{-2s_{1}+1/2}(-\tau_{2})^{-2s_{2}+1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Big(2s_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\Big)\Big(2s_{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\Big)\Gamma\Big[s_{1}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},s_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},s_{2}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2},s_{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}\Big].

(116)

Then, following the same procedure as before, we calculate each component of the longitudinal vector seed integral, and find the following results. Again, we only present the result for
r_{1}<r_{2}
, and the result for
r_{1}>r_{2}
can be obtained by switching the variables
(r_{1},p_{1})\leftrightarrow(r_{2},p_{2})
.

First, the opposite-sign integral is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\pm\mp}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}-p_{2})/2}}\,}{4\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$})}\bigg[{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{1}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{1}}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\bigg[{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{2}}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg],

(117)

where we have defined:

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{p}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r)\equiv

\displaystyle~r^{3/2+p}\bigg\{\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)\Gamma\Big[-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,p+\displaystyle\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,r^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\frac{\,{r^{2}}\,}{2}\Gamma\Big[-1-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,p+\displaystyle\frac{\,{7}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{7}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{p}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{9}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
2+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,r^{2}\right]\bigg\}.

(118)

Second, the factorized part of the same-sign integral is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}}\,}{4\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$})}\bigg[e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{1}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{1}}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\bigg[{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+{\mathbf{H}}^{p_{2}}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}}\,}{2}\Big)^{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg].

(119)

Finally, the time-ordered part of the same-sign integral is:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO},>}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}}\,}{2(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$})}r_{1}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{12}}

\displaystyle\times\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big(2n_{1}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)\Big(2n_{2}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{1}}(\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{F}}\left[\begin{matrix}2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,2n_{12}+p_{1}+p_{2}+3\\
2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\right]+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(120)

Again, we can sum up all SK components, and separate the result into a background piece, a local-signal piece, and a nonlocal-signal piece:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}=\pm}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(121)

More explicitly, the local signal is given by:

{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{L},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{D}^{p_{1}p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{H}^{p_{1}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\mathbf{H}^{p_{2}}_{-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.},

(122)

where

\mathcal{D}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\equiv\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$})}\Big[\cos\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}-p_{2})\big)-\sin\big(\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}+p_{2}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big)\Big].

(123)

The nonlocal signal is given by:

{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{NL}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{D}^{p_{1}p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{4}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{H}^{p_{1}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1})\mathbf{H}^{p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{2})+\text{c.c.},

(124)

Finally, the background is given by:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{BG},>}^{(L)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}}\cos\big[\mbox{$\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}$}(p_{1}+p_{2})\big]r_{1}^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{2n_{12}}

\displaystyle\times\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Big(2n_{1}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)\Big(2n_{2}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{1}}(\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{-n_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{F}}\left[\begin{matrix}2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,2n_{12}+p_{1}+p_{2}+3\\
2n_{2}+p_{2}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,-\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\right]+\text{c.c.}.

(125)

4.3
Summary

After the rather long and technical calculations, it would be useful to summarize and present the result we have obtained in a self-contained manner. In this section, we have computed the vector seed integral (
43
) for all three polarizations
\lambda=\pm,L
and for arbitrary power indices
(p_{1},p_{2})
. For general applications, it is more convenient to present these results with the SK indices summed. As we have shown before, the summed integral can be broken into three pieces, the background piece
\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}
, the local signal

\mathcal{I}_{\text{L}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}
, and the nonlocal signal
\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}
, according to their analytic properties at
r_{1,2}=0
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}=\pm}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{L}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(126)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\theta(r_{2}-r_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},>}^{(\lambda)p_{2}p_{1}}(r_{2},r_{1})\theta(r_{1}-r_{2}),

(127)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{L}}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(\lambda)p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})\theta(r_{2}-r_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},>}^{(\lambda)p_{2}p_{1}}(r_{2},r_{1})\theta(r_{1}-r_{2}),

(128)

Below we summarize the results for each of the three polarizations.

Transverse integrals.

The two transverse integrals correspond to
\lambda=h=\pm
. They are dependent on the chemical potential. For convenience, we introduced new variables
u_{1}=2r_{1}/(1+r_{1})
and
u_{2}=2r_{2}/(1+r_{2})
. Then, the three integrals in (
126
) can be written as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)p_{1}p_{2}}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi(p_{1}+p_{2})/2}}\,}{2^{3+p_{1}+p_{2}}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n_{12}}}\,}{n_{1}!n_{2}!}u_{1}^{n_{12}+3+p_{1}+p_{2}}

(140)

Tree-level signal cutting rule.

At the end of this section, we briefly comment on the relation between our calculation and a recently proposed cutting rule for computing CC signals at the tree level
[
41
]
. With our notations, it was proposed in
[
41
]
that the oscillating signals in inflation correlators are contributed by two sources: 1) The opposite-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\mp}
; 2) the factorized part of the same-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{F}}
. On the other hand, the time-ordered part of the same-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm,\text{TO}}
do not contribute to the signal. The upshot is that the computation of CC signals involves no genuine time-ordered integral, hence the name cutting rule. A cutting rule of this sort can be phenomenologically useful, since the computation of factorized integrals are almost always much simpler than that of the time-ordered integrals. The cutting rule proposed in
[
41
]
was based on physical arguments of particle production in the bulk, and was further checked with numerical evaluations. Our results in this paper agree with the physical observations made in
[
41
]
, and can be viewed as an analytical derivation for the cutting rule in the presence of the chemical potential. (The dS covariant case without chemical potential can be verified with previously known results
[
20
]
.) Therefore, the partial MB representation can be used to give a more rigorous and more satisfactory derivation of the cutting rule for the CC signals. We shall pursue such a proof in a future work.

5  Bootstrapping Helical Correlators

In this section we provide an alternative derivation for both the scalar seed integral (
55
) introduced in Sec.
3
and the vector seed integral (
43
) introduced in Sec.
2
, using a “bootstrapping” method developed in recent years by several groups of authors
[
20
,
22
,
23
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
25
,
32
,
33
]
. See also
[
21
]
for a recent review.

At the conceptual level, the cosmological bootstrap program aims at building inflation correlators directly at the future boundary without doing bulk time integral at each interaction vertex. This is achieved by exploiting the symmetry properties of the inflation correlators, together with a set of constraints and boundary conditions from the bulk unitarity, locality, as well as the bulk initial condition. When boiled down to a practical problem of computing inflation correlators, the bootstrap program suggests that we can derive a set of differential equations satisfied by the correlators, so that we can find the correlators by solving the differential equations instead of computing bulk integrals. The boundary conditions that are required to uniquely pin down the solution can be found from physical considerations in the bulk. For simplicity, we will collectively call such differential equations satisfied by the correlators the
bootstrap equations
, although its meaning is a bit shifted from the original conformal bootstrap program in conformal field theories.

The idea of solving the inflation correlators from a set of differential equations has been put forward originally in
[
19
]
. There, the authors get the signal part of the whole correlator, which corresponds to a solution to the homogeneous equation. The background solution, corresponding to a particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation, was later obtained in
[
20
]
. In
[
20
]
it was further shown that the bootstrap equation for the 4-point tree diagram with massive exchange can be derived from the Ward identity of dS isometries. The bootstrap program was subsequently generalized to the case of boost-breaking theories for purely massless correlators
[
29
,
30
]
and for correlators involving massive exchange
[
32
,
33
]
.

Of course, when the dS boosts are broken by the background, the bootstrap equations are not directly from the Ward identities, although we suspect that they can be related to the Ward identity with additional symmetry breaking terms. Nonetheless, one can still derive the bootstrap equations for the correlators using the very observation originally made in
[
19
]
. The essential idea is the following. The tree-level exchange digram for the 4-point correlator can be expressed as SK integrals with the massive propagator in the integrand. As the Green function for the corresponding massive field, the massive propagator solves the field equation with either a
\delta
-function source or no source. Therefore, if we insert the differential operator for the field equation in front of the massive propagator in the SK integral, the integral will be reduced to either 0 or a much simpler integral over a single time variable. Then, given that the factors multiplied to the massive propagators (including the bulk-to-boundary propagators and the coupling factors) are simple power functions or exponential functions, it is very often possible to commute the differential operator in front of the massive propagator with the integral itself, resulting in an differential equation for the whole integral, which is the desired bootstrap equation.

This bootstrap program was recently generalized to boost-breaking theories with massive exchanges
[
32
,
33
]
. These works allowed for non-unit sound speed of the massive particle and the external massless states, as well as more general boost-breaking couplings. However, their results are not directly applicable to cases with helical chemical potential, owing to the more complicated mode function for helical spinning fields, which require either a direct manipulation of Whittaker functions, or a resummation of infinite number of boost-breaking insertions in the propagator. We highlight the difference between the two cases in Figs.
4
and
5
.

The presentation below will be separated into 3 subsections, corresponding to the case of scalar change, transverse vector change, and longitudinal vector change, respectively. The procedure will be identical: We first derive the bootstrap equation, and then try to solve it. The final result will be the sum of a particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation and a solution to the homogeneous equation with proper boundary conditions. We determine the coefficients of the homogeneous solutions by imposing boundary conditions at the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
. We then check our results by looking at the folded limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 1
. Along the way, we will also get new analytical results for the 3-point and 2-point functions in completely closed analytical form.

Figure 4:
An illustration of boostless bootstrap program developed in
[
32
,
33
]
, remade from a similar figure in
[
32
]
. In this plot, the upper blue line represents the future boundary of dS and the space below represents the bulk. The blob represents the summed vertices with arbitrary number of background value
\dot{\phi}_{0}
insertions, represented by crosses. Note that, while the number of crosses are arbitrary, they are all inserted at the two vertices. The propagators in these figures can have non-unit sound speed, but this can be implemented by simple change of the variable
k\rightarrow c_{s}k
.

Figure 5:
An illustration of helical inflation correlators from resumming chemical-potential insertions. The blue wiggly line on the left hand side represents the massive spin-1 propagator dressed by the chemical potential, and the black wiggly lines on the right hand side represent the dS covariant spin-1 propagator without chemical potentials. While each gray blob already contains arbitrary number of inflaton background insertions, there are further boost-breaking insertions attached to the black propagator. A nonperturbative treatment of chemical potential effects requires summing over all such insertions. Technically, this resummation is done by directly solving the equation of motion.

5.1
Scalar

Again we begin with the known example of a massive scalar exchange. We do not pursue the most general results in this section. Instead, we will use a specific example to illustrate the idea of bootstrap equations. For this purpose, we derive a set of differential equations satisfied by the scalar seed integral (
55
) with
p_{1}=p_{2}=-2
. This corresponds to the four external states being directly coupled conformal scalars. The results for other values of
(p_{1},p_{2})
can be obtained by either following the same procedure, or acting appropriate differential operators on the result with
p_{1}=p_{2}=-2
.

Recall that the scalar seed integral with
p_{1}=p_{2}=-2
is:
