\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv-\mathsf{ab}\,k_{s}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}}\,}{\tau_{1}^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}}\,}{\tau_{2}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(141)

One useful observation here is that the following combination,

\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}(k_{s}\tau_{1},k_{s}\tau_{2})\equiv k_{s}^{3}D_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(142)

depends on the three variables
k_{s}
,
\tau_{1}
, and
\tau_{2}
only through the combinations
k_{s}\tau_{1}
and
k_{s}\tau_{2}
. Therefore, if we further define
z_{1}=k_{12}\tau_{1}
and
z_{2}=k_{34}\tau_{2}
, then the seed integral (
141
) can be rewritten in the following form:

\displaystyle r_{1}r_{2}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv-\mathsf{ab}\,\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(143)

where again
r_{1}=k_{s}/k_{12}
and
r_{2}=k_{s}/k_{34}
.

To derive an equation satisfied by the integral (
143
), we act the Klein-Gordon operator to the massive scalar propagator
D_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
, which, by definition, annihilates the opposite-sign propagator
D_{\pm\mp}
and reduces the same-sign propagator
D_{\pm\pm}
to a
\delta
function:

\displaystyle(\tau_{1}^{2}\partial_{\tau_{1}}^{2}-2\tau_{1}\partial_{\tau_{1}}+k_{s}^{2}\tau_{1}^{2}+m^{2})D_{\pm\mp}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=0,

(144)

\displaystyle(\tau_{1}^{2}\partial_{\tau_{1}}^{2}-2\tau_{1}\partial_{\tau_{1}}+k_{s}^{2}\tau_{1}^{2}+m^{2})D_{\pm\pm}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\mp\mathrm{i}\tau_{1}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}).

(145)

These equations can be rewritten as equations satisfied by the “hatted” propagator
\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})
:

\displaystyle(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2})\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\mp}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=0,

(146)

\displaystyle(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2})\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\pm}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=\mp\mathrm{i}(r_{1}z_{1})^{2}(r_{2}z_{2})^{2}\delta(r_{1}z_{1}-r_{2}z_{2}).

(147)

Now we insert the operator
r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2}
in front of
\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}
in the integral (
143
), and use the above two equations, which give rise to the following two equations:

\displaystyle+\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\big(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2}\big)\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\mp}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=0,

(148)

\displaystyle-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\big(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2}\big)\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\pm}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{(r_{1}r_{2})^{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(149)

At this point, if we can somehow pull the differential operator
r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}+m^{2}
out of the integral, then we will get an differential equation for the whole integral. This can be done by using the following two relations:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\,z_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-1)\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(150)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\,z_{1}^{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=

\displaystyle-(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}})(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-1)\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}).

(151)

These relations are direct consequences of integration by parts. For instance, the first relation (
150
) can be derived as follows:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\,\partial_{z_{1}}\Big[z_{1}^{-1}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})\Big]=\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\,\Big[-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}}\,}{z}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\Big]e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(152)

and the second relation (
151
) can be derived similarly.

Then, using (
151
), we find that the integral
r_{1}r_{2}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})
in (
143
) satisfies the equations:

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{9}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]\Big(r_{1}r_{2}\mathcal{I}_{\pm\mp}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big)=0,

(153)

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{9}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]\Big(r_{1}r_{2}\mathcal{I}_{\pm\pm}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big)=\displaystyle\frac{\,{(r_{1}r_{2})^{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(154)

From these equations it is direct to get the following equations for the seed integral itself, which turns out a bit simpler than the above ones:

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}^{3}\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\pm\mp}(r_{1},r_{2})=0,

(155)

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-2r_{1}^{3}\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\pm\pm}(r_{1},r_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(156)

These are the bootstrap equations satisfied by the scalar seed integral, to which we shall derive a proper solution satisfying all required boundary conditions.

Particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation.

We first try to find a particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation (
156
). Following the method originally used in
[
20
]
, we use double series expansion in both
r_{1}
and
r_{1}/r_{2}
as an ansatz and try to solve the series coefficients. To this end, we assume
r_{1}<r_{2}
, and Taylor expand the right hand side of the inhomogeneous equation (
156
):

\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}=r_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{n}.

(157)

The form of this series motivates us to try the following ansatz for the inhomogeneous solution:

\mathcal{Z}(r_{1},r_{2})=r_{1}\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}r_{1}^{m}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{n},

(158)

Substituting the ansatz (
158
) and the expanded source term (
157
) back into the inhomogeneous equation (
156
), and matching the powers of
r_{1}
and
r_{1}/r_{2}
, we find the following set of recursion relations satisfied by the series coefficients
\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}
:

\displaystyle\Big[\big(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{Z}_{0,n}=

\displaystyle~1;

(159a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{1,n}=

\displaystyle~0;

(159b)

\displaystyle\Big[\big(m+n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{Z}_{m+2,n}=

\displaystyle~(m+n+1)(m+n+2)\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}.

(159c)

This recursion relation is straightforward to solve with the general term given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{2m,n}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+1)(n+2)}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}}\cdots\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+2m-1)(n+2m)}\,}{(n+2m+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}},

(160)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{2m+1,n}=

\displaystyle~0,

(161)

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{2m,n}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+1)_{2m}}\,}{4^{1+m}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{2m+1,n}=0.

(162)

Note that the inhomogeneous solution we have found applies to both
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{++}
and
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{--}
. Furthermore, this solution is analytic in both
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
as
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
, and thus contributes only to the background piece of the 4-point correlator. As we shall see soon, the homogeneous solutions are all nonanalytic in
r_{1,2}
at
r_{1,2}=0
, and therefore the above particular solution is the only contribution to the background. Therefore we conclude that the background piece of the scalar seed integral
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})
is twice of the above particular solution, namely:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n}(n+1)_{2m}}\,}{2^{2m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}}r_{1}^{2m+1}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{n}.

(163)

This recovers the same result originally found in
[
20
]
. Following the same procedure, we can also derive the background part for other choices of
p_{1,2}
. In Fig.
6
we show the results for this series for both
p_{1}=p_{2}=-2
and
p_{1}=p_{2}=0
, together with the results (
3.3
) from the partial MB representation, for fixed
r_{2}=0.5
. From this figure we can see that both methods yield convergent results for most of values of
r_{1}
, and they agree well with each other. We also observe that the results from partial MB representation have better convergence speed in the case of
p_{1}=p_{2}=0
, which corresponds to a realistic case with derivatively coupled external massless scalars. Therefore, we see that the partial MB result could be advantageous for numerical implementation at least in some cases.

Figure 6:
The summed scalar seed integrals
\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})
as functions of
r_{1}
with fixed
r_{2}=0.5
and
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu=2
. For the two cases
p_{1}=p_{2}=-2
(left) and
p_{1}=p_{2}=0
(right), we show the convergences of series from partial MB representation (
3.3
) and from the bootstrap method (
163
), by including terms of
m
and
n
summations up to values indicated in the plots.

Homogeneous solutions and boundary conditions.

The full result for the scalar integral should be the sum of the above particular solution (
163
) with a solution to the corresponding homogeneous equation (
155
). It is straightforward to solve (
155
) to get the following pair of independent solutions:

\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}(r_{1})=r_{1}^{1/2}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Gamma\Big[\mp\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big]{}_{2}\mathrm{F}_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}\pm\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$},\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{4}$}\pm\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$}\\
1\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,r_{1}^{2}\right]=\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{F}_{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{-2}(r_{1}),

(164)

where
\mathbf{F}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p}(r)
is the function defined in (
78
).
Here we have multiplied an
r_{1}
-independent coefficient for later convenience.
Since
\mathcal{Y}_{-}(r_{1})=\mathcal{Y}_{+}^{*}(r_{1})
, the general symmetric and real homogeneous solution can be written as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{Y}_{+}(r_{1})\Big[\alpha_{++}\mathcal{Y}_{+}(r_{2})+\alpha_{+-}\mathcal{Y}_{-}(r_{2})\Big]+\text{c.c.},

(165)

where
\alpha_{++}
and
\alpha_{+-}
are two arbitrary complex coefficients to be determined. This homogeneous solution is in fact the signal piece because of the non-analytical behaviors around
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
and thus we add a subscript S to
{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{S},>}^{-2,-2}
. The full correlator is then:

{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{>}(r_{1},r_{2})={\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(166)

To determine the coefficients
\alpha_{++}
and
\alpha_{+-}
, we need to impose boundary conditions, which are usually found by considering some special limits of
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{>}(r_{1},r_{2})
in
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
. In previous works, the folded limits
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 1
and the factorized limits
r_{1,2}\rightarrow-1
were used to impose boundary conditions. Here let us adopt a different choice: We shall consider the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
, keeping
r_{1}\ll r_{2}
, and determine the coefficients by comparing the leading terms with the bulk integral.

In this squeezed limit, it is easy to check that the leading behaviors:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})\rightarrow

\displaystyle~\mathcal{O}(r_{1}),

(167)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2})\rightarrow

\displaystyle~(r_{1}r_{2})^{1/2}\bigg\{\alpha_{++}\Gamma^{2}(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma^{2}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{4}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+\alpha_{+-}|\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)|^{2}\Big|\Gamma\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\Big)\Big|^{2}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg\}+\text{c.c.},

(168)

so the background contribution can be neglected.

On the other hand, we consider the same squeezed limit for the bulk integral, which is equivalent to taking the late-time limits for the propagators:
\tau_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
with
\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
. So the (anti-)time-ordered propagators become much simpler:

\displaystyle D_{\pm\pm}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\rightarrow D_{\mp\pm}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(169)

By comparing the result of the bulk integral (See Sec.
3
), we can finally determine the coefficients:

\alpha_{++}=\alpha_{+-}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1+\mathrm{i}\sinh\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2\pi}.

(170)

With these two coefficients determined, we reach the full solution (
166
) to the bootstrap equation with appropriate boundary conditions. This completes the bootstrap for the scalar seed integral.

Folded limit and alternative version of scalar bootstrap equation.

Now let us check the folded limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
of the above bootstrapped solution (
166
). Our choice of Bunch-Davies initial condition for all fluctuations requires that the solution (
166
) is regular in the folded limit. However, both the signal and the background pieces are divergent in this limit. The cancellation of this superficial divergence is thus a useful consistency check of our result.

It is relatively complicated to analyze the behavior of the background piece in the folded limit with the expression (
163
). Thus, inspired by the transverse vector integral derived in Sec.
4
, we introduce two new variables
u_{1,2}=2r_{1,2}/(1+r_{1,2})
as in (
44
), and rewrite the (inhomogeneous) scalar bootstrap equation (
156
) as:

\bigg[(u_{1}^{2}-u_{1}^{3})\partial_{u_{1}}^{2}-u_{1}^{2}\partial_{u_{1}}+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\pm\pm}(u_{1},u_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}u_{2}}\,}{u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{1}u_{2}},

(171)

where we have rewritten
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\pm\pm}
as a function of
u_{1,2}
.
Again, we Taylor expand the right hand side:

\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}u_{2}}\,}{u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{1}u_{2}}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u_{1}^{n+1}\Big(1-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n},

(172)

and try the following ansatz which is analytical for both
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
(and thus both
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
):

\mathcal{X}_{\pm}(u_{1},u_{2})=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{X}_{m,n}u_{1}^{m+n+1}\Big(1-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n}.

(173)

Substituting the ansatz and the Taylor expansion back into the bootstrap equation, and matching the coefficients for each term, we obtain the following recursion relations:

\displaystyle\Big[\big(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{X}_{0,n}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2};

(174a)

\displaystyle\Big[\big(m+n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{X}_{m+1,n}=(m+n+1)^{2}\mathcal{X}_{m,n},

(174b)

and the solution can be expressed as:

\mathcal{X}_{m,n}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+1)_{m}^{2}}\,}{2(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}},

(175)

and finally the background piece is the twice of our solution, but now in terms of
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
:

{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n}(n+1)_{m}^{2}}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}u_{1}^{m+1}(1-u_{2})^{n}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n}.

(176)

It is worth noting that the expression (
176
) is equivalent to (
163
), and we will use (
176
) to analyze the background behavior in the folded limit.

Without loss of generality, we first consider the limit
r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
.
For the background piece, notice that
u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
when
r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
and thus only
n=0
terms survive due to the factor
(1-u_{2})^{n}
. Therefore, the background can be written in closed form in this limit:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(1)_{m}^{2}}\,}{(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}u_{1}^{m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}\times{}_{3}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,1\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,u_{1}\right],

(177)

which converges for all
0\leq r_{1}<1
. This implies the divergences in the homogeneous solution (the signal part) must be canceled with each other. Indeed, the divergent parts for the homogeneous solutions are:

\lim_{r\rightarrow 1^{-}}\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}(r)\sim\mp\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}}\text{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\log(1-r),

(178)

and thus these divergences get canceled in the combination:

\lim_{r\rightarrow 1^{-}}\Big[\mathcal{Y}_{+}(r)+\mathcal{Y}_{-}(r)\Big]=\sqrt{2\pi^{3}}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu).

(179)

Therefore, the signal converges in the folded limit for
0\leq r_{1}<1
:

\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\sqrt{2\pi^{3}}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\alpha_{++}\mathcal{Y}_{+}(r_{1})+\text{c.c.}.

(180)

Now we go on to take the second folded limit
r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}
. This time, both the background and the signal diverge, but the divergences are canceled in the finally result:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\mathcal{I}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},1^{-})\sim

\displaystyle-\pi\,\text{sech}\log(1-u_{1})\sim-\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\log(1-r_{1}),

(181)

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},1^{-})\sim

\displaystyle~\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\log(1-r_{1}).

(182)

After the cancellation of divergences, we get a finite result in the double folded limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{>}(1^{-},1^{-})=

\displaystyle~2\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\text{Re}\,\Big[\big(1+\mathrm{i}\,\text{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big)\psi\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)+\gamma\Big]

\displaystyle~+\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
2,2,2\end{matrix}\middle|\,1\right],

(183)

where
\psi(z)={\mathrm{d}}\log\Gamma(z)/{\mathrm{d}}z
is the digamma function. When supplemented with appropriate
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
-independent prefactors (including the coupling and the momentum), this result is nothing but the 2-point correlator of massless scalar fields
\varphi
corrected by an intermediate massive scalar
\sigma
of mass parameter
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
with coupling
a^{3}\varphi^{\prime}\sigma
. This result has been derived originally in
[
15
]
; See also
[
58
]
. Although our result has a different look from the expressions in
[
15
,
58
]
, we have checked numerically that the two results perfectly agree with each other.

Thus we have shown that the full correlator is regular in both the single folded limit (
0\leq r_{1}<1
,
r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
) and the double folded limit (
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
), as expected.

5.2
Transverse vector

Next we consider the transverse components
(\lambda=h=\pm)
of the vector seed integral (
43
), following the same procedure as before. Again we do not pursue the general case, but only focus on an example with
p_{1}=p_{2}=-1
. Generalizations to arbitrary
p_{1,2}
should be straightforward.

The goal here is to derive a differential equation for the following seed integral:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle-\mathsf{ab}k_{s}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}}\,}{\tau_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}}\,}{\tau_{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D^{(h)}_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathsf{ab}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{(h)}_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(184)

where we have defined
z_{1}\equiv k_{12}\tau_{1}
,
z_{2}\equiv k_{34}\tau_{2}
. In the second line, we have introduced the hatted propagator for the transverse vector fields, which is again a function of
k_{s}\tau_{1}
and
k_{s}\tau_{2}
:

\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{(h)}_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s}\tau_{1},k_{s}\tau_{2})\equiv k_{s}D^{(h)}_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(185)

Then, the equation of motion for the transverse spin-1 fields (
14
) gives rise to the following equations satisfied by the propagators,

\displaystyle(\tau_{1}^{2}\partial_{\tau_{1}}^{2}+k_{s}^{2}\tau_{1}^{2}-2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0muk_{s}\tau_{1}+m^{2})D^{(h)}_{\pm\mp}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=0,

(186)

\displaystyle(\tau_{1}^{2}\partial_{\tau_{1}}^{2}+k_{s}^{2}\tau_{1}^{2}-2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0muk_{s}\tau_{1}+m^{2})D^{(h)}_{\pm\pm}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\mp\mathrm{i}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}),

(187)

which further imply the following equations for the hatted propagators:

\displaystyle(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}-2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mur_{1}z_{1}+m^{2})\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\mp}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=0,

(188)

\displaystyle(r_{1}^{2}\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}+r_{1}^{2}z_{1}^{2}-2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mur_{1}z_{1}+m^{2})\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\pm\pm}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=\mp\mathrm{i}(r_{1}z_{1})(r_{2}z_{2})\delta(r_{1}z_{1}-r_{2}z_{2}).

(189)

We can now again insert the above differential operators in front of the hatted propagator in (
5.2
), using the following
relations similar to (
150
) and (
151
) to commute the differential operator with the integral:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\,z_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}})\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(190)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\,z_{1}^{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})=

\displaystyle-(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}+1)(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}})\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(191)

and we find the following equations satisfied by the integral:

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-(\pm 2\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mur_{1}^{2}+2r_{1}^{3})\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\mp}(r_{1},r_{2})=0,

(192)

\displaystyle\bigg[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{4})\partial_{r_{1}}^{2}-(\pm 2\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mur_{1}^{2}+2r_{1}^{3})\partial_{r_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm}(r_{1},r_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(193)

It is possible to solve this set of equation directly, with a set of homogeneous solutions and a particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation. The resulting inhomogeneous solution has a double-series representation, with the coefficients satisfying a set of recursion relations that can be easily found. However, it turns out not trivial to find a general term formula from this set of recursion relations. Thus it is not easy to write down the explicit series solution in the same way as we did in (
163
). Fortunately, the previous computation with partial MB representation in Sec.
4
has hinted at a solution: We have learnt that the change of variables
u_{1,2}=2r_{1,2}/(1+r_{1,2})
as in (
44
) can further simplify the equations. With the new variables
u_{1,2}
, we finally reach the following set of vector bootstrap equations that are much easier to solve:

\displaystyle\bigg[(u_{1}^{2}-u_{1}^{3})\partial_{u_{1}}^{2}-(1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}^{2}\partial_{u_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})=0,

(194)

\displaystyle\bigg[(u_{1}^{2}-u_{1}^{3})\partial_{u_{1}}^{2}-(1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}^{2}\partial_{u_{1}}+\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}\Big)\bigg]{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}u_{2}}\,}{u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{1}u_{2}}.

(195)

Particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation.

Now we look for a particular solution to the inhomogeneous equation (
195
). As before, we first Taylor expand the right hand side of (
195
) as a series of
u_{1}
, already given in (
172
),
and consider the following ansatz for the particular solution:

\mathcal{V}_{\pm}^{(h)}(u_{1},u_{2})=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{V}_{\pm;m,n}^{(h)}u_{1}^{m+n+1}\Big(1-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n},

(196)

where the subscript in
\mathcal{V}_{\pm}^{(h)}(u_{1},u_{2})
corresponds to the two choices of the SK indices in
{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm}^{(h)}
. (Unlike the scalar equation, the inhomogeneous equations for the vector seed integral are different for
{\mathcal{I}}_{++}^{(h)}
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{--}^{(h)}
.)
Compare the coefficient for fixed
n
and we find:

\displaystyle\Big[\big(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{V}_{\pm;0,n}^{(h)}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2};

(197a)

\displaystyle\Big[\big(m+n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}\big)^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}\Big]\mathcal{V}_{\pm;m+1,n}^{(h)}=(m+n+1)(m+n+1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathcal{V}_{\pm;m,n}^{(h)}.

(197b)

This recursion relation is easy to solve, with the solution given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\pm;m,n}^{(h)}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+1)(n+1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}}\cdots\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+m)(n+m\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{(n+m+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$})^{2}+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{(n+1)_{m}(n+1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m}}\,}{2(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}.

(198)

As in the scalar solution considered before, this particular solution is the sole contribution to the background of the vector seed integral. Therefore, the full result for the background is obtained by adding up
\mathcal{V}_{+}^{(h)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{-}^{(h)}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\text{BG}}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n}(n+1)_{m}(n+1\pm\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m}}\,}{2(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}u_{1}^{m+1}(1-u_{2})^{n}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n}+\text{c.c.}.

(199)

In Fig.
7
, we show the background series
\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG}}^{(h)-1,-1}
in (
199
) together with the partial MB results (
4.3
). Again we see that both methods yield convergent results with good agreements. Note that, for the parameters chosen in the figure, the signals are much greater than the background. Therefore we only show the background part of the vector seed integral in this figure, since the purpose here is to show the convergence of the background series.

Figure 7:
The background part of the vector seed integrals
\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG}}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})
as functions of
u_{1}
with fixed
u_{2}=0.5
. We show the convergences of series from partial MB representation (
4.3
) and from the bootstrap method (
199
), by including terms of
m
and
n
summations up to values indicated in the plots.

Homogeneous solutions and limits.

Next we consider the homogeneous solution. Note that, unlike the previous scalar case, the homogeneous and inhomogeneous equations are different for
\mathcal{I}_{+\pm}^{(h)-1,-1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{-\mp}^{(h)-1,-1}
.
The general solution to the upper-sign equation in (
194
) is the linear combination of
\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})
and
\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})
, while the general solution to the lower-sign equation is the linear combination of
\mathcal{U}^{*}_{+}(u_{1})
and
\mathcal{U}^{*}_{-}(u_{1})
, where
\mathcal{U}_{\pm}(u_{1})
is given by:

\mathcal{U}_{\pm}(u_{1})=u_{1}^{1/2\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1\pm 2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,u_{1}\right]=\mp\mathrm{i}\displaystyle\frac{\,{\sqrt{2}}\,}{\pi}\sinh(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{G}^{-1}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1}).

(200)

Here
\mathbf{G}_{h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p}(u)
is the function defined in (
98
).
Note that we have expressed these solutions in terms of the dressed hypergeometric function (
277
) for later convenience.
Furthermore, since amplitudes with different SK indices obey different equations, we can separately determine the homogeneous solutions for each
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathsf{ab}}^{(h)-1,-1}
by comparing the leading terms in the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
with
r_{1}\ll r_{2}
.

We first focus on the opposite-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\mp}
. On one hand,
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{+-}(u_{1},u_{2})
satisfies the homogeneous equation (
194
)
and thus must be a linear combination of
\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})
and
\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})
. On the other hand, from (
5.2
) we notice that
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{+-}(u_{1},u_{2})=\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{-+}(u_{2},u_{1})
, which also satisfies (
194
) but with the variable
u_{2}
.
This implies that
\mathcal{I}_{+-}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})
should also be a linear combination of
\mathcal{U}_{+}^{*}(u_{2})
and
\mathcal{U}_{-}^{*}(u_{2})
. Therefore, we can write
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\mp}
as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{+-}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})\Big[\beta_{++}\mathcal{U}^{*}_{-}(u_{2})+\beta_{+-}\mathcal{U}^{*}_{+}(u_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle~+\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})\Big[\beta_{-+}\mathcal{U}^{*}_{-}(u_{2})+\beta_{--}\mathcal{U}^{*}_{+}(u_{2})\Big],

(201)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{-+}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1,*}_{+-}(u_{1},u_{2}),

(202)

We then consider the same-sign integrals
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm}
. Similar to the scalar case,
\mathcal{I}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++}
is the combination of an inhomogeneous solution (corresponding to the background piece) to (
195
), and a factorized homogeneous solution denoted by
{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm,\text{F},>}
. Furthermore, from (
5.2
) we can see that
{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++}(u_{1},u_{2})={\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++}(u_{2},u_{1})
, which further implies that
{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++,\text{F},>}(u_{1},u_{2})
can be written as a linear combination of
\mathcal{U}_{\pm}(u_{1})
, as well as a linear combination of
\mathcal{U}_{\pm}(u_{2})
. Therefore we can write:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++,\text{F},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})\Big[\gamma_{++}\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{2})+\gamma_{+-}\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle~+\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})\Big[\gamma_{-+}\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{2})+\gamma_{--}\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{2})\Big],

(203)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{--,\text{F},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1,*}_{++,\text{F},>}(u_{1},u_{2}).

(204)

To determine the coefficients, we again consider the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
with
r_{1}\ll r_{2}
and compare the behaviors of homogeneous solutions with the late-time approximation of the bulk integrals. Again, in the squeezed limit, the background piece

{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})\rightarrow~\mathcal{O}(u_{1})
and thus can be neglected. Comparing homogeneous solutions to the bulk integrals (See Sec.
4
), we find:

\displaystyle\beta_{++}=-\beta_{+-}=-\beta_{-+}=\beta_{--}=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{4}\text{csch}^{2}(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)(\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\cosh 2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu),

(205)

\displaystyle\gamma_{++}=-\gamma_{+-}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}\pi e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\text{csch}^{2}(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)],

(206)

\displaystyle\gamma_{--}=-\gamma_{-+}=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}\pi e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\text{csch}^{2}(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2\Gamma[\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}e^{-\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\cosh[\pi(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)],

(207)

and the full amplitude is given by:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{>}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\Big[{\mathcal{I}}_{+-}^{(h)-1,-1}(u_{1},u_{2})+{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{++,\text{F},>}(u_{1},u_{2})+\mathcal{V}_{+}(u_{1},u_{2})\Big]+\text{c.c.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\bigg\{\Big[\big(\gamma_{++}\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})-\gamma_{--}\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})\big)-\beta_{++}\big(\mathcal{U}^{*}_{+}(u_{1})-\mathcal{U}^{*}_{-}(u_{1})\big)\Big]\Big(\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{2})-\mathcal{U}_{-}(r_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{V}_{+}(u_{1},u_{2})\bigg\}+\text{c.c.}.

(208)

This completes the bootstrap of the transverse vector seed integral.

Folded limit.

Now we consider the folded limit
u_{1,2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
. First we take
u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
. The background piece is regular in this limit:

\displaystyle\lim_{u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\mathcal{V}_{+}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{V}_{+;m,0}^{(h)}u_{1}^{m+1}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}\times{}_{3}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathrm{F}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,1+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,u_{1}\right].

(209)

So, any divergences in the signals must cancel among themselves. The divergent parts for the homogeneous solutions are:

\displaystyle\lim_{u\rightarrow 1^{-}}\mathcal{U}_{\pm}(u)\sim-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi\text{csch}(h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\Gamma(1-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}(1-u)^{-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu},

(210)

we find that this divergence is canceled in the following combination:

\displaystyle\lim_{u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\Big[\mathcal{U}_{+}(r_{2})-\mathcal{U}_{-}(r_{2})\Big]=\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi^{2}\text{csch}(h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big(\text{sech}[\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]-\text{sech}[\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]\big)}\,}{\Gamma(1+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}.

(211)

On the other hand, the signal part in the folded limit
u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
can be written as

\displaystyle\lim_{u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}(u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Big[\big(\gamma_{++}\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{1})-\gamma_{--}\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{1})\big)-\beta_{++}\big(\mathcal{U}^{*}_{+}(u_{1})-\mathcal{U}^{*}_{-}(u_{1})\big)\Big]\lim_{u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\Big[\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{2})-\mathcal{U}_{-}(r_{2})\Big]+\text{c.c.},

(212)

which is therefore convergent, as expected. For later use, we also give an explicit expression for the signal in
u_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
limit:

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}(u_{1},1^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{\sinh(h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\text{csch}(2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\sqrt{2}\pi}

\displaystyle\times\Big[(e^{-2\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+e^{-2h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu})\displaystyle\frac{\,{\Gamma(\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}-\mathrm{i}e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(1+e^{-2\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)})\displaystyle\frac{\,{\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\Big]

\displaystyle\times u_{1}^{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg\{\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathbf{G}^{-1}_{-h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})+\mathrm{i}e^{\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Gamma(\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\mathbf{G}^{-1}_{+h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(u_{1})\bigg\}+\text{c.c.}.

(213)

We then consider the double folded limit, by further taking
u_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}
. Like the case of scalar correlator considered before, both the background and the signal diverge in this limit. The background diverges as

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},1^{-})\sim-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(1-u_{1})^{-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\text{c.c.},

(214)

while the signal diverges as

\displaystyle\lim_{u_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}(u_{1},1^{-})\sim

\displaystyle~(\gamma_{++}-\gamma_{-+})\displaystyle\frac{\,{-\mathrm{i}\pi\text{csch}(h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{\Gamma(1-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}(1-u_{1})^{-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\times\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\Big[\mathcal{U}_{+}(u_{2})-\mathcal{U}_{-}(u_{2})\Big]+\text{c.c.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\pi\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(1-u_{1})^{-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+\text{c.c.},

(215)

so the full correlator is regular in the double folded limit as well. After the cancellation, the finite result in the double folded limit is:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(h)-1,-1}_{>}(1^{-},1^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi\text{sech}^{2}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\,}{2h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\bigg\{\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}(1-e^{-2h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu})+\displaystyle\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\cosh\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{\Gamma[\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu]}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\pi e^{-h\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\Gamma(-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)-\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
1-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,1-\mathrm{i}h\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,1\right]\bigg)\bigg\}+\text{c.c.}.

(216)

The readers might have found it a bit unphysical to take the folded limit of the transverse seed integral. After all, the transverse component of the massive spin-1 exchange as shown in Fig.
1
does not survive the folded limit. (For instance, the
r_{2}\rightarrow 1
limit means that either
\mathbf{k}_{s}
is parallel to
\mathbf{k}_{4}
or
\mathbf{k}_{4}\rightarrow 0
. In either case, the contraction
\mathbf{k}_{4}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\mathbf{k}_{s}}^{(\pm)^{*}}
in (
2.2
) vanishes.) However, there do exist cases where the folded limit of transverse integrals does not vanish, if we consider tensor external states. We will show such an example in Sec.
6
, where the above folded limit can be directly used.

5.3
Longitudinal vector

Finally we consider the longitudinal component
(\lambda=L)
of the vector seed integral (
43
) with
p_{1}=p_{2}=-1
:

{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1},r_{2})=-\mathsf{ab}\,k_{s}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}}\,}{\tau_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}}\,}{\tau_{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}D^{(L)}_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(217)

Similar to our computation with partial MB representation in Sec.
4
, here we can recycle the result for the scalar bootstrap equation. Note again that the longitudinal propagator
D^{(L)}_{>}
is related to the massive scalar propagator
D_{>}
by (
115
). For convenience, we define the operator:

\mathcal{D}_{i}\equiv\partial_{\tau_{i}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{2}\,}{\tau_{i}}.\qquad(i=1,2)

(218)

Then, (
115
) implies the following relations for the longitudinal propagators
D_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(L)}
and the scalar propagator
D_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}
of the same mass parameter
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
(but not the same mass):

\displaystyle D_{\pm\mp}^{(L)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}D_{\pm\mp}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),

(219)

\displaystyle D_{\pm\pm}^{(L)}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\Big[\theta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}D_{\gtrless}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})+\theta(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}D_{\lessgtr}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\Big[\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}D_{\pm\pm}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\mp\mathrm{i}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\Big].

(220)

Note in particular the appearance of a contact term
\delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})
in the last line of (
5.3
), which is a consequence of commuting the operator
\mathcal{D}_{1,2}
with the Heaviside
\theta
-functions in
D_{\pm\pm}
. See (in particular, App. B of)
[
58
]
for more discussions about this contact term.

Using the relations (
219
) and (
5.3
), we can directly obtain the longitudinal vector seed integral by acting differential operators on the scalar seed
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{-2,-2}
in (
143
). More explicitly,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\pm\mp}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}}\,}{m^{2}}(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-2)(r_{2}\partial_{r_{2}}-2)\Big(r_{1}r_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big),

(221)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}}\,}{m^{2}}(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-2)(r_{2}\partial_{r_{2}}-2)\Big(r_{1}r_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})\Big)+\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{r_{1}r_{2}}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(222)

We can rewrite (
221
) and (
222
) as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\pm\mp}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\mp}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2}),

(223)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{\pm\pm}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2})+\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg].

(224)

In these equations we have used the relation
m^{2}=\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4
, and defined:

\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{i}\equiv r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}-1.~~~~~(i=1,2)

(225)

Summing over all SK indices, we can collectively write
{\mathcal{I}}_{>}^{(L)-1,-1}\equiv\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{(L)-1,-1}
in terms of the scalar hatted integral
{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{>}\equiv\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}
, as:

{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{>}(r_{1},r_{2})+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg].

(226)

Intermediate steps.

Here we provide the intermediate steps leading to (
221
) and (
222
). Uninterested readers can skip this part. The strategy is to compute the following integral:

\displaystyle-\mathsf{ab}\times\displaystyle\frac{\,{k_{s}}\,}{m^{2}}\times\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}}\,}{\tau_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}}\,}{\tau_{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}\Big(\partial_{\tau_{1}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{2}\,}{\tau_{1}}\Big)\Big(\partial_{\tau_{2}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{2}\,}{\tau_{2}}\Big)D_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathsf{ab}\times\displaystyle\frac{\,{(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}}\,}{m^{2}}\times\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-2}\,}{z_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{2}\partial_{r_{2}}-2}\,}{z_{2}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\times(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}\times(r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}}-2)(r_{2}\partial_{r_{2}}-2)\times(-\mathsf{ab})\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}}\,}{z_{1}^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}}\,}{z_{2}^{2}}\,e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}z_{2}}\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2}),

(227)

where
D_{\mathsf{ab}}(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
in the first line denotes the scalar propagator, and the hatted propagator
\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}
is defined in (
142
). Similar to previous cases, we use the variables
z_{1}=k_{12}\tau_{1}
,
z_{2}=k_{34}\tau_{2}
. In the second line we substitute
z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}
with
r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}
acting on
\mkern 2.0mu\widehat{\mkern-2.0muD\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{\mathsf{ab}}(r_{1}z_{1},r_{2}z_{2})
.
Finally, the contact term for
\mathcal{I}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\pm\pm}
arising from the delta-function is:

-\displaystyle\frac{\,{k_{s}}\,}{m^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}}\,}{\tau_{1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}}\,}{\tau_{2}}\,\Big[\mp\mathrm{i}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\delta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\Big]e^{\pm\mathrm{i}k_{12}\tau_{1}\pm\mathrm{i}k_{34}\tau_{2}}=-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{m^{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}.

(228)

Combining all results above, together with the definition of seed integrals, we can directly get (
221
) and (
222
).

Solutions.

The above derivations show that the longitudinal correlator can be obtained by acting the operator (
225
) on the scalar correlator given in (
166
), together with an extra contact term which is regular when
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
and thus contributes to the background piece. Again, the longitudinal correlator can be separated into a homogeneous part (the signals) and a inhomogeneous particular solution (the background). We work them out in turn. Acting (
225
) on the homogeneous solution solutions to the scalar integral, we find a pair of solutions:

\mathcal{W}_{\pm}(r_{1})=\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}(r_{1})=-\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{2}\Big)^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\mathbf{H}^{-1}_{\pm\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1}),

(229)

where
\mathbf{H}^{p}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}
is defined in (
4.2
). The homogeneous solution to the longitudinal bootstrap equation is thus a linear combination of these two solutions with appropriate boundary conditions:

\mathcal{I}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}=\mathcal{W}_{+}(r_{1})\Big[\delta_{++}\mathcal{W}_{+}(r_{2})+\delta_{+-}\mathcal{W}_{-}(r_{2})\Big]+\text{c.c.}.

(230)

Similar to the scalar bootstrap, we compare the solution in the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
with the late-time limit of the bulk integral. This determines the coefficients to be:

\delta_{++}=\delta_{+-}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1+\mathrm{i}\sinh\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4)}.

(231)

One can also obtain the same result by simply acting the operator
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}/(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4)
on (
165
).
For the inhomogeneous solution (the background), we apply the differential operator to (
163
), and add the contact term:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{1}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}{\mathcal{I}}^{-2,-2}_{\text{BG},>}(r_{1},r_{2})+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-1)^{n+1}(n+1)_{2m}(2m+n)(n+1)}\,}{2^{2m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{n}\,}{2}$}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{4}$}-\mbox{$\frac{\,{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\,}{2}$})_{m+1}}r_{1}^{2m+1}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{r_{1}}\,}{r_{2}}\Big)^{n}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg].

(232)

Note that the last contact term is crucial for us to obtain the correct result for the background. This completes the derivation of the longitudinal correlator from the bootstrap method.

Folded limit.

Finally let us check the folded limit of the longitudinal correlator.
For simplicity, we again express the background piece in terms of
u_{1,2}
, namely inserting (
176
) into the first line of (
5.3
):

\displaystyle~{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}_{1}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mathcal{D}\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu_{2}\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{[(n+1)_{m}]^{2}}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}u_{1}^{m+n+1}\Big(1-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1/4}\bigg[\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{-(n+1)_{m}^{2}}\,}{(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}(n+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)_{m+1}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(m+n-r_{1})}\,}{(1+r_{1})}\displaystyle\frac{\,{n+1-r_{2}}\,}{1-r_{2}}

\displaystyle\times u_{1}^{m+n+1}\Big(1-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{u_{2}}\Big)^{n}+\displaystyle\frac{\,{2r_{1}r_{2}}\,}{r_{1}+r_{2}}\bigg].

(233)

We first take
r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}
, then only terms with
n=0
and
n=1
survive, giving:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},u_{2})=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{u_{1}}\,}{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1}\bigg\{4+u_{1}-\Big(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{7}\,}{4}\Big)\pi\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)u_{1}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,1\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,u_{1}\right]-(2-u_{1})\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}2,2,2\\
\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|\,u_{1}\right]\bigg)\bigg\},

(234)

where
\mathcal{F}
is the dressed (generalized) hypergeometric function defined in (
277
).
We find the background piece converge for all
0\leq r_{1}<1
. On the other hand, the homogeneous solutions diverge:

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow 1^{-}}\mathcal{W}_{\pm}(r_{1})\sim\pm\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{2}}\text{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big[\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{1-r}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7}\,}{8}\log(1-r)\Big],

(235)

but the combination:

\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}\Big[\mathcal{W}_{+}(r_{2})+\mathcal{W}_{-}(r_{2})\Big]=\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi^{3/2}}\,}{2^{5/2}}(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)

(236)

converges. Therefore,

\lim_{r_{2}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},r_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi^{3/2}}\,}{2^{5/2}}(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\delta_{++}\mathcal{W}_{+}(r_{1})+\text{c.c.},

(237)

and thus the signal also converges for all
0\leq r_{1}<1
.

We then take the double folded limit by also taking
r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}
. Again, both the background and the signal diverge, but the divergences are canceled:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{1}\rightarrow 1^{-}}{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{BG},>}(u_{1},1^{-})\sim

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)}\,}{4(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1)}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big[\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{1-u_{1}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7}\,}{4}\log(1-u_{1})\Big],

(238)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)}\,}{2(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1)}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big[\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{1-r_{1}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7}\,}{8}\log(1-r_{1})\Big],

(239)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{\text{S},>}(r_{1},1^{-})\sim

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)}\,}{2(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1)}\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big[\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{1-r_{1}}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7}\,}{8}\log(1-r_{1})\Big],

(240)

which shows that the longitudinal correlator is regular in the folded limit, as expected.
More explicitly,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(L)-1,-1}_{>}(1^{-},1^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{5}\,}{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{3}\,}{16}(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\frac{\,{(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7)^{2}}\,}{8(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1)}\pi\,\text{sech}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\,\text{Re}\,\Big[\big(1+\mathrm{i}\,\text{csch}(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\big)\psi\big(\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big)+\gamma\Big]

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\frac{\,{4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}-7}\,}{4(4\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{2}+1)}\bigg\{\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
2,2,2\end{matrix}\middle|\,1\right]-\mathcal{F}\left[\begin{matrix}1,1,\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mbox{$\frac{\,{5}\,}{2}$}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\\
3,3,3\end{matrix}\middle|\,1\right]\bigg\}.

(241)

Again, this result can be directly used to express the 2-point function mediated by a massive longitudinal spin-1 field.

6  Variations: Higher Spins and Bispectra

In this work, we have been mainly focusing on the 4-point correlators with spin-1 exchange. Nevertheless, it is straightforward to generalize our methods and results to other cases, such as the chemical potential for higher spin fields
(s\geq 2)
and the 3-point function. As an example, in this section, we shall briefly consider the 3-point function mediated by a massive spin-2 field with a helicity-dependent chemical potential. Note that the 3-point functions with massive exchange such as in Fig.
3
and Fig.
8
are formally obtained by taking
k_{4}\rightarrow 0
in corresponding 4-point functions. In this limit,
k_{s}=k_{3}
, and thus
r_{2}=u_{2}=1
. Thus taking the 3-point limit in the seed integrals is formally equivalent to taking the folded limit
r_{2}\rightarrow 1
. The way we take folded limits of 4-point functions in Sec.
5
allows us to find closed analytical results for the 3-point functions without any series expansion. Thus we also take this chance to present a similar closed-form analytical expression for the well-studied scale-exchange diagram (Fig.
3
), which seems new to us.

Helical chemical potential for higher spins.

Before presenting the details, we first make two general remarks.

The first remark is about arbitrary nonzero spin. In principle, the chemical potential reviewed in Sec.
2
has a natural generalization to massive fields of any nonzero spin. Let us consider integer spins for simplicity, and we can describe massive spin-
s
states by a rank-
s
symmetric tensor
\sigma_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{s}}
. A helicity-dependent chemical potential for this field can thus be introduced from the following dimension-5 coupling between
\sigma_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{s}}
and a background scalar field
\phi

[
89
]
:

\mathcal{O}^{(s)}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2\Lambda}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\phi\nabla_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu\lambda_{2}\cdots\lambda_{s}}\nabla_{\rho}\sigma_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda_{2}\cdots\lambda_{s}}.

(242)

In particular, for a massive spin-2 field
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
, we have:

\mathcal{O}^{(2)}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2\Lambda}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\phi\nabla_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu\lambda}\nabla_{\rho}\sigma_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}.

(243)

Then, with the rolling background
\langle\phi\rangle=\dot{\phi}_{0}t+\text{const.}
, these operators generate terms proportional to the particle number operator weighted by the helicity. Unlike the chemical potential operator for spin-1 field in (
2
), the above operators break the gauge symmetry for the corresponding massless spin-
s
fields. This does not place any obstacle, since the gauge symmetries are absent for massive fields anyway. In fact, for the spin-2 case, one might consider an alternative way of introducing the chemical potential via the operator
\phi\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R_{\mu\nu\kappa\lambda}R_{\rho\sigma}{}^{\kappa\lambda}
where
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
is the Riemann tensor associated with
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
(but not with the spacetime metric
g_{\mu\nu}
). This operator is indeed capable of generating a helicity-dependent chemical potential for the spin-2 field in the perturbative regime, as has been extensively studied in the literature. However, it is known that this operator is pathologic in the UV
[
111
]
, and our mechanism to boost the particle production (and thus the inflation correlators) works exactly at or beyond the UV cutoff scale of this operator. Therefore, we shall only consider (
243
). See
[
89
]
for more discussions.

The second remark is about the 3-point function. In a 3-point function with a massive spinning particle exchange at the tree level, it is necessary that the intermediate massive particle is attached to the external inflaton fluctuation through a two-point mixing. Then the angular momentum conservation at this two-point vertex implies that only the longitudinal component of the spinning particle can make nonzero contributions. On the other hand, the helicity-dependent chemical potential only boosts transverse components of the massive spinning particles, and does absolutely nothing to the longitudinal component. Therefore, we do not expect to see the chemical potential boosted helical correlators in tree-level 3-point functions. (It is possible to generate chemical potential boosted 3-point function at one-loop as has been studied in the literature
[
61
,
75
,
70
,
71
,
79
]
.) However, there is a unique chance where we can see the effect of the helical chemical potential in tree-level 3-point functions. That is, we can consider a mixed correlator
\langle\varphi\varphi\gamma\rangle
among two external inflaton fluctuations
\varphi
and a tensor mode
\gamma
. Then, we can form a two-point mixing between the massless tensor mode
\gamma_{\mu\nu}
and the massive spin-2 field
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
. Due to the spin-2 nature of the external tensor mode, this two-point mixing will pick out the helicity-2 component of
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
rather than the longitudinal component. Therefore, there is a chance that this mixed operator is boosted by the chemical potential. This example has been considered in
[
89
]
, in which the signal part of the mixed 3-point function has been calculated. With our results from previous sections, we can now easily derive the full analytical expression for this process.

Figure 8:
The helical 3-point correlator (
248
). The straight lines represent external inflaton modes, the black doubly wiggly line represents the massless tensor mode, and the blue doubly wiggly line represents the massive spin-2 field with helical chemical potential.

In this section, we shall focus only on the helicity-2 states of the massive spin-2 field, which is the dominant contribution to the inflation correlators when there is a nonzero chemical potential.
For the massive spin-2 field
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
of mass
m
and with a chemical potential term (
243
), the mode equation corresponding to the helicity-2 component
\Sigma^{(\pm 2)}(k,\tau)
reads:

\Sigma^{(\pm 2)}{}^{\prime\prime}-2a\Sigma^{(\pm 2)}{}^{\prime}+\Big[k^{2}\pm 2a\mu k+a^{2}(m^{2}-2)\Big]\Sigma^{(\pm 2)}=0,

(244)

where
\mu\equiv\dot{\phi}_{0}/\Lambda
is the chemical potential. From this equation, we find the properly normalized mode function as (Our convention for normalizing the mode function and the polarization tensors is in accordance with
[
89
]
.)

\Sigma^{(\pm 2)}(k,\tau)=-\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{\mp\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu/2}}\,}{2\sqrt{k}\tau}\mathrm{W}_{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(2\mathrm{i}k\tau),

(245)

where
\mathrm{W}_{\mu,\nu}(z)
is again the Whittaker W function, and
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\equiv\mu/H
and
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\equiv\sqrt{m^{2}/H^{2}-9/4}
. (Here we temporarily restore the Hubble parameter
H
. Note that the definition of the mass parameter
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu
differs from the rest of the paper, due to the spin of the particle. In general, for a spin-
s
state with
s\neq 0
, the mass parameter is defined by
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\equiv\sqrt{m^{2}/H^{2}-(s-1/2)^{2}}
, and for
s=0
, it is defined by
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\equiv\sqrt{m^{2}/H^{2}-9/4}
.)

With the above mode function, we can construct the transverse spin-2 propagator as in previous sections:

D^{(\pm 2)}_{>}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2\tau_{1}\tau_{2}}\displaystyle\frac{\,{e^{\mp\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}}\,}{2k}\mathrm{W}_{\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(2\mathrm{i}k\tau_{1})\mathrm{W}_{\mp\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\mu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(-2\mathrm{i}k\tau_{2}).

(246)

Mixed bispectrum.

To form a 3-point function at the tree level, we need a two-point mixing between the massive spin-2 field
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
and the massless tensor mode
\gamma_{ij}
, as well as 3-point vertex connecting
\sigma_{\mu\nu}
with two massless inflaton fluctuations
\varphi
. In accordance with
[
89
]
, we introduce the following couplings:

\Delta{\mathscr{L}}=\lambda_{2}a\gamma_{ij}^{\prime}\sigma_{ij}-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\lambda_{3}\sigma_{ij}\partial_{i}\varphi\partial_{j}\varphi.

(247)

Then, the three-point function shown in Fig.
8
